# WHITEPILL: every video prompt

The record of every video generation made for WHITEPILL, the seventh Claudia music film by anabology: the previz takes of MiniMax H3, the reshoots of Wan 3.0 Prime, the tests and masters of Seedance 2.5 and Kling 4.0, with the image edits and shader renders that fed them. Each prompt is given word for word ([…] marks a cut); where many prompts share a template, the template is given once and each job its own parts. It was made 18–22 October 2026. The director's words are quoted as typed.

anabology, before any picture existed: "instead of seeing any animation we make at all on screen, it all being some pipeline like: 1- midjourney edit gets a prompt that defines the general composition with our image and it edits on a beautiful (but illegible) animation composition 2- we repaint over the composition with code and animate it 3- we re-light / re-render the animation with Wan 3.0 for example entirely So the end is pure video model, and all visual design decisions made by image models rather than you, but we make it precise". With the song locked: "i want you to basically shoot the entire video twice […] i think one great thing about seedance is that you can take something thats already been made, and use seedance 2.5 to re-render the entire scene with way more description […]". The film ended as that idea turned around: a previz performer in a green balaclava (H3), then Seedance 2.5 drawing every shot of hers from words, with Suno's own vocal stem for the lip sync, and the worlds without her written as shaders and repainted by Seedance.

**Prices.** Costs are at list price, from the project's ledgers. Higgsfield's API (Wan 3.0 Prime and Seedance 2.5): the ledger's estimate per request, at one rate a second for the output and the input video alike: Wan 3.0 Prime at $0.068, $0.14 and $0.28 a second at 480p, 720p and 1080p, Seedance 2.5 at 0.6 × $0.2056, $0.4622 and $1.1372. The ledger did not count the input video's seconds until section 7; entries before it give that figure too, "with the input video's seconds". Replicate (Seedance 2.5): per output second, $0.1028 at 480p without a video input and $0.4304 with one, $0.9676 at 720p with one; Replicate does not bill a request that fails or is refused, though the ledger lists it. The takes of Seedance 2.5 on Higgsfield (References), her shots in the final shoot, and of Kling 4.0 on Higgsfield are given in seconds. MiniMax H3's cost is not recorded. Image edits (GPT Image 2.5) as the image API reported them.

**The order of work.** The song locked and the video asked for; the first H3 takes (section 1); the go for the whole film, H3 then Wan 3.0 Prime over it (sections 1 and 3); the first half on the song (section 3); the rollout cut (section 5); the reset, with the shader worlds and the film on the bar map (section 6); previz 15 approved for the shoot (section 1) and the Wan cut (section 7); the Suno-stem method (section 8); the look chosen (section 9); the final shoot, v1, then v2a and v2b, then the final cut, v3 (section 10).

**How to read an entry.** The heading is the job's name (and "in the final cut" where it is); then the model, length, resolution, sound and cost ("submitted twice" where a job went out twice); then its inputs, its take or takes, the cuts it played in (v1, v2a, v2b, v3), and what came of it. In prompts, ⟨NAME⟩ is a slot of the template above it, ⟦W1⟧ a shared paragraph printed once at the start of its section, and ⟨…⟩ a run of sentences unchanged from the prompt it is compared with. The references a prompt names are the model's own tags: @Image1 and @Video1 in Seedance on Higgsfield, [Image1] on Replicate, Image 1 and Video 1 in Wan, `<Picture 1>` in H3, [Image 1] in Kling.

**Sections.** 1. MiniMax H3: the previz performer · 2. Over the first takes: Seedance and Wan as a fix pass · 3. Wan 3.0 Prime: the first film pass · 4. Take 8: animation, lyric stills and grey boxes · 5. Wan 3.0 Prime: the rollout · 6. The reset: a shader world through Wan · 7. Wan 3.0 Prime: v10, every shot reshot · 8. S01 five ways · 9. Replicate: her face refused, and the Wes Anderson look tests · 10. The final shoot · 11. The stills that fed the video jobs · 12. The shader scenes.

## 1. MiniMax H3: the previz performer

MiniMax H3 was used in its reference-to-video-with-audio mode: reference pictures (`<Picture 1>` …), at most one reference video used for motion only (`<Video 1>`, a dancer) and one audio track (`<Audio 1>`) that the take copies as its soundtrack while her lips follow it (`fully_copy`). Every take was 1344 × 768 at 24 fps, with a seed; the longest were 15.08 s. A cut detector checked every take, and only takes without a hard cut were kept (anabology, when a Wan pass kept a take's cuts: "ok it still cut so we'll need to just do h3 til we get them without cuts"). The prompts use the structured format of MiniMax's prompt guide for H3: `subject_definitions`, `summary`, `retention_analysis`, `detailed_description` (ending in `[Shot 1]`), `overall_soundscape`, `non_diegetic_music`; the sung words sit in `<d>[English] …</d>`. Every take was previz: none is in the final cut, but the v10 takes were the low-resolution guides of the first Seedance shoot (section 10), and nine of the takes they guided are in the final cut (S06, S07, S11, S13, S14, S15, S22, S24, S25).

anabology, before the first take, on the camera: "One thing I love is - can you look at the camera movement long continuous shot tests? I want the most of those we can (+lip sync) - like I love really creative many-angled shots, the camera should feel like it's on an industrial robotic arm with how unreal the camera movements should be. Show me some tests of that in context […] so we can align".

**The batches**, in the order they ran, counted from the takes kept in the project and the cut detector's results. "Cut" means the detector found a hard cut in the take.

### Set 1 and the tests before the film

References: `<Picture 1>`–`<Picture 4>` four close-up crops of her face, `<Picture 5>` the Midjourney plate of the place (mirrored so her streak falls on the canon side); `<Audio 1>` the song's vocal for the window, from the project's own stem separation. The chorus take (`cha`, 15.08 s, from song 73.59 s) in full:

```
subject_definitions:
<Subject 1> is the woman whose face, hair and thin headset microphone come from <Picture 1>, <Picture 2>, <Picture 3> and <Picture 4>, four photographs of the same woman, and are kept exactly as pictured, including her glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs and the single clay-orange streak in her bangs on her right side; she also wears a small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip above her left ear. The clothing and backgrounds of those four pictures are not used.
<Subject 2> is her outfit: the sleeveless cream ribbed-knit top with a high round neck from <Picture 5>, worn with high-waisted coral-red wide-leg trousers and a thin coral belt.
<Subject 3> is the place from <Picture 5>: a little white rounded tram car standing on a single narrow rail among the rows of an apple orchard full of red apples, in low golden sunlight, inside an enormous space colony whose land curves up on both sides and closes overhead. The woman's face and hair in <Picture 5> are not used.
<Audio 1> is the sung lead vocal of <Subject 1> (S1), a soft, breathy, intimate female pop voice singing four short lines over silence, and it is reused 1:1 as the complete final audio track.

summary:
[reference generation + audio reuse] The 15.08-second target video is one single continuous take with no cuts: <Subject 1> wearing <Subject 2> stands beside the white tram in the orchard of <Subject 3> at golden hour and sings <Audio 1>, while the camera, mounted on a high-speed industrial robotic arm, flies around her through many very different angles with impossible machine precision.

retention_analysis:
<Subject 1> (appears in [Shot 1]): fully_preserved - her face, her hair with its single clay-orange streak on her right side, the star clip above her left ear and the headset microphone are retained exactly as in the pictures, rendered as a natural full-colour photograph.
<Subject 2> (appears in [Shot 1]): fully_preserved - the cream knit top and the coral trousers are retained.
<Subject 3> (appears in [Shot 1]): fully_preserved - the white tram, the orchard and the land curving overhead are retained.
<Audio 1>: fully_copy - <Audio 1> is reused 1:1 as the target video's complete final audio track, and the mouth movements of <Subject 1> follow its sung lines.

detailed_description:
The target video is in a live-action, cinematic music-video style shot on 35mm film with fine grain and warm 1970s colour, in low golden sunlight with soft haze. It is one single continuous take with no cuts. The camera is mounted on a high-speed industrial robotic motion-control arm: it moves with an impossible, machine-precise speed and smoothness that no human operator could achieve. Every move whips fast between two viewpoints and then decelerates smoothly to a dead, precise stop, with no shake, jitter or wobble anywhere in the take.
[Shot 1] The take opens on an extreme close-up of her lips and the thin headset microphone as she begins to sing. The arm whips back and up in one fast arc to a high top-down view looking straight down on her and the white roof of the tram among the green orchard rows, and stops precisely. It swoops down in a fast spiral around her and decelerates into a low angle at her knees, looking up past her face to the land curving overhead, where white towns, orchards and wheat fields hang upside down in the hazy sky. It snaps sideways in a fast lateral slide to her profile and stops exactly on the star clip in her hair. It rolls the horizon a quarter turn as it orbits behind her and around her left side, then lands in a frontal medium close-up as she sings straight into the lens. Finally it races backward along the rail and rises into a wide shot where she is a small figure beside the tram, with the whole curving world above her, and holds there. Throughout, <Subject 1> (S1) sings every line of <Audio 1> with her lips, jaw and cheeks moving in exact sync with every syllable of the copied track: <d>[English] Skynet woke up folding socks. Wake up, babe, new ending dropped. Universal high income. The most likely outcome.</d> She sings with calm, warm, amused confidence, swaying gently with the rhythm, and her mouth closes in the short pauses between the lines.

overall_soundscape:
<Audio 1> is reused 1:1 as the complete final audio track, carrying her sung lines and the silence around them exactly as recorded. No wind, footsteps, whoosh or other sounds are added.

non_diegetic_music:
No score is added; <Audio 1> remains the complete final audio track.
```

The other tests use the same format. Each entry gives its new subject lines and its `[Shot 1]`, and names the lines already shown; its summary, retention and soundscape lines restate the subjects in H3's terms, as cha's do. The beat tests also describe the camera as "dancing to the music at 124 beats per minute: one beat lasts 0.48 seconds and one bar of four beats lasts 1.93 seconds", and the green-screen plate's description is a VFX studio with "a regular grid of small white cross-shaped tracking markers".

**WP_int (the intro, 8.00 s, seeds 1–2)**: seed 1 cut at 0.29 s, seed 2 cut-free. <Subject 1> as in cha.

```
<Subject 2> is her outfit from <Picture 5>: a sleeveless cream linen summer dress with a thin brown leather belt.
<Subject 3> is the place from <Picture 5>: a curved cream-white bedroom with a low bed of white linen and one huge round window, through which green fields, orchards and white towns climb up and curve overhead inside an enormous space colony, under long strips of window letting in morning sun. The woman's face and hair in <Picture 5> are not used.
[Shot 1] The take opens on an extreme close-up of one of her eyes as it opens. The arm whips back and up over the bed in one fast arc to a top-down view looking straight down on her hands as they fold a pair of white socks into a neat square on her lap, and stops precisely. It then sweeps low and fast past the huge round window, where the land curves up and overhead in the morning haze, and swings around to land in a frontal medium close-up of her as she turns to the lens. Throughout, <Subject 1> (S1) sings every line of <Audio 1> with her lips, jaw and cheeks moving in exact sync with every syllable of the copied track: <d>[English] Skynet woke up folding socks. Wake up, babe, new ending dropped.</d> She sings the second line straight into the lens with a small, amused, knowing smile, and her mouth closes in the pause between the lines.
```

**WP_wpl (the white-pill lines, 8.00 s, seeds 1–2)**: both cut-free. <Subject 1> as in cha.

```
<Subject 2> is her outfit from <Picture 5>: a sleeveless cream ribbed-knit top with a high round neck and a cream knee-length skirt with a coral belt.
<Subject 3> is the place from <Picture 5>: a vast meadow of golden wheat and coral poppies at golden hour, where two giant glossy capsule-shaped sculptures, each several storeys tall, stand upright in the field, a blue one on the left and a red one on the right, with the woman standing in the field between them. The woman's face and hair in <Picture 5> are not used.
[Shot 1] The take opens high above the meadow on a perfectly symmetrical view: the blue capsule on the left, the red capsule on the right, and <Subject 1> a small figure in the wheat exactly between them. The arm dives straight down the centre line at high speed and decelerates to a precise stop on a frontal medium shot of her as she sings. It whips in a fast half-orbit around her, the giant capsules sliding past behind her, and stops on a low angle looking up at her as she raises her open palm with a small white pill on it toward the lens. It then glides in to a close-up of her face beside the white pill as she sings the last line straight into the lens. Throughout, <Subject 1> (S1) sings every line of <Audio 1> with her lips, jaw and cheeks moving in exact sync with every syllable of the copied track: <d>[English] Red pill, blue pill. I'm the white pill. Smarter than you, love you still.</d> She sings with calm, warm, amused confidence, and her mouth closes in the short pauses between the lines.
```

**WP_v1 (the verse at the stone zero, 8.00 s, seeds 1–2)**: both cut-free. <Subject 1> as in cha.

```
<Subject 2> is her outfit from <Picture 5>: a sleeveless cream cable-knit top with a high round neck and high-waisted coral-red wide-leg trousers.
<Subject 3> is the place from <Picture 5>: a vast sunlit meadow of golden wheat and coral poppies under a hazy cream sky, where giant weathered white stone monuments stand: on the left a huge carved stone ring in the shape of the numeral zero, taller than a person, with a black leather jacket hanging from its top, and on the right a long row of huge carved stone numerals. The woman's face and hair in <Picture 5> are not used.
[Shot 1] The take opens on a low view skimming fast just above the poppies toward her as she walks, and it decelerates to a precise stop on a frontal medium close-up as she sings the first line. The arm whips up and around her in a fast arc to a high three-quarter view looking down on her, the stone zero and the leather jacket below, and stops precisely. It then dives fast and flies straight through the hole of the giant stone zero, the rough carved stone rushing past the lens on every side, and decelerates on the far side into a close-up of her face as she turns to the lens and sings the second line with a perfectly straight, deadpan face. Throughout, <Subject 1> (S1) sings every line of <Audio 1> with her lips, jaw and cheeks moving in exact sync with every syllable of the copied track: <d>[English] Half of you fear I'll end the human race. Zero, says the leather jacket, with a straight face.</d> Her mouth closes in the pause between the lines.
```

**WP_b8ts_vox (8 s from song 73.59 s, the camera's stops as timestamps on the beat grid, the vocal as `<Audio 1>`)**: cut-free. <Subject 1> as in cha; <Subject 3> as in cha.

```
<Subject 2> is her outfit: the white sleeveless ribbed tank top from <Picture 5>, worn with high-waisted orange wide-leg trousers and a thin orange belt.
[Shot 1] From 00:00.000 to 00:01.900, the camera whips in a fast arc from her left profile around to the front of her face and stops dead in a close-up exactly on the downbeat at 00:01.900. From 00:01.900 to 00:03.830, without any cut, the same move continues: on that downbeat the arm snaps straight up to a high top-down view of her whole body standing on the front of the white tram among the orchard rows, bouncing slightly closer on every beat, and stops dead at 00:03.830. From 00:03.830 to 00:05.760, still in the same unbroken move and twice as fast, the camera dives in two quick drops that land on the beats at 00:04.790 and 00:05.760, ending on a low angle at her knees looking up past her face to the land curving overhead. From 00:05.760 to 00:07.690, without any cut, the camera orbits half way around her in the opposite direction and stops dead in a frontal close-up exactly on the downbeat at 00:07.690, then holds still on her face to the end.
```

**WP_b8ts_mix (the same with the full mix as `<Audio 1>`, seeds 1–2)**: 2 cuts at seed 1, 1 cut at seed 2. <Subject 1> as in cha; <Subject 2> as in beat8_ts_vox; <Subject 3> as in cha; [Shot 1] as in beat8_ts_vox.

**WP_b8free_mix (no timestamps, "dance to the beat", the full mix)**: cut-free. <Subject 1> as in cha; <Subject 2> as in beat8_ts_vox; <Subject 3> as in cha.

```
[Shot 1] The camera dances to the beat of the music in <Audio 1>: on every downbeat (every 1.93 seconds) it changes direction, and between downbeats it whips fast and stops dead exactly on the beat. It whips from her profile to a close-up of her face, snaps straight up to a high top-down view of her whole body standing on the front of the white tram among the orchard rows, dives in two quick drops to a low angle at her knees looking up at the land curving overhead, and orbits half way around her in the opposite direction to end on a frontal close-up of her face.
```

**WP_chags (the chorus as a green-screen plate, seeds 1–2; no place picture)**: cut-free.

```
<Subject 1> is the woman whose eyes, eyebrows, lips and the skin around them come from <Picture 1>, <Picture 2>, <Picture 3> and <Picture 4>, four photographs of the same woman. In the target video she is a performer in a visual-effects suit: a skin-tight, matte chroma-key green full-body suit with a matte green hood that covers her head, her hair, her ears and her neck completely; only her eyes and her lips show through two openings in the hood. The hair, clothing and backgrounds of those four pictures are not used.
[Shot 1] The take opens on an extreme close-up of her lips through the opening of the green hood as she begins to sing. The arm whips back and up in one fast arc to a high top-down view looking straight down on her whole body standing on the front of the green stand-in box, the white tracking crosses on the green floor around her, and stops precisely. It swoops down in a fast spiral around her and decelerates into a low angle at her knees, looking up past her face at the green ceiling. It snaps sideways in a fast lateral slide to her profile and stops exactly beside her head. It rolls the horizon a quarter turn as it orbits behind her and around her left side, then lands in a frontal medium close-up as she sings straight into the lens, her eyes moving from the distance to the lens. Finally it races backward along the green rail and rises into a wide shot where she is a small green figure on the box in the middle of the green studio, and holds there. The beat tests (camera stops found by optical flow and matched to the beat grid, `tools/h3/cambeat.py`): H3 made the moves in the order written but mostly not at the written times; in the two best takes 4 of 9 and 6 of 10 stops landed within 80 ms of a beat, against about 1 in 3 by chance; the full mix as `<Audio 1>` put more stops on the beat but added hard cuts; the vocal-only take jumped to another outfit and set in its last 1.5 s; "dance to the beat" alone was at chance. The green-screen plate kept her motion but gave the redraw nothing to stand on; anabology, after its redraw (section 2), asked for "the greenscreen model but in the real world, dancing, moving, etc - doing the entire thing". The dance clips were clips of adult dancers from public dance videos, cut on a downbeat and re-timed to the song's tempo; two of the first three were dropped on review.

### The film in 21 long takes

anabology's go, after the dance tests: "[…] i approve that longer version - all h3 […] previz then wan 3.0 prime reshoots of the entire video post animation. remember, describe the animation lighting changes in depth - for example (untextured) -> liquid glass, or (untextured / black) -> hippie scrapbook art etc. etc. (dont need to use those just themes you can describe) and define how it's re-lit specifically, e.g. the lighting effects (shoudl add lighting effects). Go ahead."

The song was cut into 21 long takes, T01–T21, each starting on a beat in a vocal gap (`tools/film/takes.py`, `tools/film/gen_h3.py`). The performer was a green-suit stand-in with only her eyes and lips showing, so that the video model would draw her face later. References: the same four face crops, the take's Midjourney plates as `<Picture 5…>`, a dance clip as `<Video 1>` on the dance takes (T02 and T07 disco steps; T08, T12 and T18 a 1970s disco class), the vocal window as `<Audio 1>`. T08's prompt as rendered (kept by the audit of the performer):

```
subject_definitions:
<Subject 1> is the woman whose eyes, eyebrows, lips and the skin around them come from <Picture 1>, <Picture 2>, <Picture 3> and <Picture 4>, four photographs of the same woman. In the target video she is a performer in a visual-effects suit: a skin-tight, matte chroma-key green full-body suit with a matte green hood that covers her head, her hair, her ears and her neck completely; only her eyes and her lips show through two openings in the hood. The hair, clothing and backgrounds of those four pictures are not used.
<Subject 2> is the place from <Picture 5>: a vast meadow of golden wheat and coral poppies at golden hour where two giant glossy capsule-shaped sculptures, each several storeys tall, stand upright in the field, a blue one on the left and a red one on the right. Any person shown in <Picture 5> is not used.

<Audio 1> is the sung lead vocal of <Subject 1> (S1), a soft, breathy, intimate female pop voice singing 4 short lines over silence, and it is reused 1:1 as the complete final audio track.

summary:
[reference generation + audio reuse] The 12.96-second target video is one single continuous take with no cuts, filmed on location in <Subject 2>: <Subject 1>, the performer in the green chroma-key suit and hood, sings and moves through the scene while the camera, mounted on a high-speed industrial robotic arm, flies around her through many very different angles with impossible machine precision.

<Subject 2> (appears in [Shot 1]): fully_preserved - the place and everything in it are retained.

detailed_description:
The target video is in a live-action, cinematic music-video style shot on 35mm film with fine grain and warm 1970s colour, in low golden sunlight with soft haze, on location; the only unusual thing in it is the performer's plain green visual-effects suit. It is one single continuous take with no cuts. Throughout, <Subject 1> (S1) sings every line of <Audio 1> with her lips and jaw moving in exact sync with every syllable of the copied track: <d>[English] The most likely outcome. Red pill, blue pill. I'm the white pill. Smarter than you, love you still.</d> Her eyes are expressive and alive, and her lips close in the pauses between the lines.

overall_soundscape:

non_diegetic_music:
No score is added; <Audio 1> remains the complete final audio track.

### Take 8: the performer and the dress

anabology, on the film's first half: "[…] i also dont feel llike her character is perfect.. ### v10: her 20 shots on the bar map

After the reset (section 6) the film was rewritten on the song's bar map (`tools/film/v10/shots.py`): 26 shots, every cut on a bar line, 20 of them hers (P) and 6 shader scenes (M). Each of her shots got one take, starting on its downbeat, with a calm camera written in the take's own seconds; a shot longer than 15.08 s went in squeezed by q = length ÷ 14.9 (the vocal sped up by q, the take slowed back by q in the cut), on the two keys anabology gave for the shaders (section 12): the shaders' compressed intricacy and the film structured on the audio from the start.

On previz 14: "ok i like it, i think that lip sync failed esp in the beginning a bit - liek, maybe you didnt include the lyrics because they were meant for the next bar? or too early or something. the pacing is great. […] it feels like maybe your prompt didnt have the right words for the entire scene […] h3 is usually pretty good at it." and "in the gens you should tell it to have a little subtle film grain". The fixes (`tools/film/v10/gen_h3_v10.py`): the prompt names exactly the words heard in the take's window, fragments marked "…", silence described in order; the vocal reference is silent before the first named word and after the last; a word sung across a cut belongs to both takes; each dancer re-timed to the shot's tempo × q; "a little subtle film grain". Seed 1 had the first prompts (whole lines); seed 11 was stopped after two takes and restarted as seed 12 with the fixes; seeds 13–17 retried S03, S04, S07, S11b, S16 and S22. The prompt files hold the generator's last output (written for S11b's seeds 16–17), so for the seed-12 picks they may differ in which words cross a cut.

At 0.0 s the camera rises straight up fast from her and decelerates smoothly into a high top-down view that reveals the giant letters mown into the wheat around her small figure, at rest by 3.8 s, and holds there.

From the project's notes: S03 cut twice between hand and face and was reframed as one medium close shot (seed 3 cut-free); S05's seed 1 had a jump the detector missed (seed 2); S19's empty wheat plate read OVER and was edited to WHITE PILL (seed 2); S23's seed 12 jumped at 10.9 s (seed 1 kept); S03 kept opening her mouth before "Half" until seed 14, whose action lost every "smile"; S11b (the line anabology found unsynced at 1:33 of previz 15) got seed 17 once its words ended at the cut. On previz 15: "this is great, lets shoot the whole thing."

**A seed does not pin an H3 take.** S03 at seed 14 gave three different takes in three runs, so the take file is the record.

## 2. Over the first takes: Seedance and Wan as a fix pass

anabology, on the first H3 set: "this is great. i want to make sure when we do the final takes, you describe her outfit (like, say keep orange pants and white shirt), give the seedance model the official character sheet/refs (attached) and you say to fix any inconsistencies/replace with the character in the refs with the outfit you describe. because, the seedance pass really should fix and h3 errors. as a test, do this with the first 5 secs of the chorus take so we can see how to make it properly fix it. can be low res as we're drafting." and "oh and make sure to say to keep the exact timing and lip movement as well from h3". Thirteen submissions over the chorus take and the plates made from it: 11 takes, one refused, one failed. Replicate's Seedance names its references [Video1] / [Image1]; Higgsfield's @Video1 / @Image1; Wan's Video 1 / Image 1. The paragraph that recurs is given once:

⟦F1⟧

Claudia is the woman in the character sheet @Image1: her face, her glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs, the one clay-orange streak through her bangs on her right side, the small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip above her left ear, the thin headset microphone at her cheek, her pale skin and freckles. The sheet only shows who she is; she wears a white sleeveless ribbed tank top and high-waisted orange wide-leg trousers with a thin orange belt.
```

The lesson the lead took (and the project notes keep): an edit or reference pass copies a realistic source in both modes; a short, loose prompt does not change that; a grey blockout makes Seedance redraw but loosens the camera. The next step was a cleaner source: the green-suit performer on location, then real dance clips as H3's motion reference (section 1).

## 3. Wan 3.0 Prime: the first film pass

anabology's plan, with the song locked: "i want you to basically shoot the entire video twice - you can previz with […] and you can master with seedance AFTER you animate. i think one great thing about seedance is that you can take something thats already been made, and use seedance 2.5 to re-render the entire scene with way more description (adn see the prompt skill of course) - like, you can animate something with solid colors that you want to come out as refracting glass, and just tell seedfance to make the animated parts refracting glass (With a lot more prompt detail). so i think if you do teh entire video twice, it will be best. you could use a cheaper model like wan 3.0 prime on higgsfield first pass then second pass seedance 2.5 to master." After the go (section 1), each H3 take of the film (section 1) went to Wan 3.0 Prime as it landed, with our code-drawn layer on top: flat, untextured shapes (letters on the field, numerals on the stones, the tower's lit windows, floating capsules) that the prompt names as real things (`tools/film/gen_wan.py`, `tools/film/anim.py`). Higgsfield's `alibaba/wan-3.0-prime/reference-to-video`, 720p 16:9, no generated audio, the duration the take's whole seconds (Wan's input video is capped at 15 s). Video 1 = the take with the layer, cut to ≤ 15 s; Image 1 = the official character sheet (B2 and B1 for the last two); Audio 1 = the take's vocal window. Every take came back at 1280 × 720, 30 fps, exactly as long as asked. Shared paragraphs:

```

Redraw the performer in the green chroma-key suit and green hood as Claudia; no green suit, green hood or green skin remains anywhere. Image 1 is Claudia's character sheet, only a reference for who she is; it is never shown as a shot.
```

Claudia is a 28-year-old Caucasian American woman with pale skin and light freckles, blue eyes, a glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs, one clay-orange streak through the bangs on the left of the frame when she faces the camera, a small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip on the other side above her ear, and a thin headset microphone at her cheek, exactly as in Image 1. In every frame she wears a white sleeveless ribbed tank top and high-waisted orange wide-leg trousers with a thin orange belt. Her movement and dancing are smooth, fluid and consistent: continuous natural motion from frame to frame, with no jitter, no sudden jumps and no warping limbs; her body, face, hair and outfit stay the same the whole time. She sings Audio 1: her lips follow Audio 1 exactly.
```

It looks like real 35 mm film photography with warm 1970s colour and fine grain, a real place filmed with a real camera.
```

## 4. Take 8: animation, lyric stills and grey boxes

Take 8 (song 84.17–96.69 s: "The most likely outcome / Red pill, blue pill / I'm the white pill / Smarter than you, love you still", the capsule meadow) was where the method was worked out. All on Higgsfield, 13 s at 720p, Audio 1 = the vocal window, Image 1 = the big front face from her face sheet and Image 2 = the A1 dress from four sides (except where noted); Video 1 = the H3 take, with or without our drawing on it. The paragraphs these prompts share with each other and with the rollout (section 5):

[Generation objective] Re-shoot Video 1 as a live-action music video: Claudia (Images 1 and 2) sings Audio 1 and dances in a golden-hour wheat field full of red poppies, between two giant glossy capsules, one blue and one red, while the lines of the song stand in the field as large, solid, three-dimensional letters at the places and times set below. One continuous take with Video 1's camera movement and timing.

[Reference assets]
- Video 1 is the shot: keep its camera path, framing and timing, the place and everything in it, the performer's dancing and every body movement, and her mouth movements. It is one continuous take with no cuts.
- Audio 1 is Claudia's own singing voice: her lips follow Audio 1 word by word.
- Image 1 is Claudia's face and Image 2 is her dress, references only for who she is and what she wears.
- Image 3 is the PLACEMENT SHEET: a plain clay layout model of this same set, seen whole from five angles (front, high front, three-quarter left, three-quarter right and from above), with every lyric and every effect in its place at once. It shows only where things are: the position, size, height, angle and spacing of each lyric and effect in the field, and the letterforms. Take nothing else from it: none of its grey floor, flat grey light, mannequin or labels appears in the video.
- Image 4 is the ORDER SHEET: the same model from the front, step by step, showing the order in which the lyrics appear.
- In both sheets: the grey mannequin is Claudia; the tall blue capsule is the giant blue capsule of Video 1 and the tall red capsule is the giant red capsule; the grey floor is the wheat field; the small pale-blue capsules are the floating glass pills; the white letters are the lyric objects (white only in the model, which shows their shape and place, not their colour); the red and blue hoops round the capsules are the rings of light; the pale ring behind the mannequin's head marks the halo.

[Subject] Redraw the performer in the green chroma-key balaclava as Claudia: no green balaclava, green fabric or green skin remains anywhere, and her face, hair and head are fully visible. Claudia is a 30-year-old Caucasian American woman with a grown-up angular face, pale skin and light freckles, blue eyes, a glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs and one clay-orange streak through the bangs, exactly as in Image 1: the streak starts at the parting on her right side, the LEFT side of the frame when she faces the camera, and the small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip sits above her left ear, on the RIGHT side of the frame; the streak and the clip are never on the same side. A thin headset microphone sits at her cheek. She is very tall and very skinny, with long thin legs, thin arms and a narrow waist. In every frame she wears exactly the dress of Image 2: a white cotton milkmaid mini dress with a ruffled, lace-edged square neckline, a fitted lace-up corset bodice with silver eyelets and a white ribbon, big puffed short sleeves with ribbon ties and lace cuffs and a full two-tier ruffled skirt with lace trim, with white leather square-toe Mary Jane shoes. She looks happy and full of joy: her eyes smile, and she smiles warmly between the sung lines. Her movement is smooth and consistent, with no jitter, no sudden jumps and no warping limbs.

[Event script] Video 1 gives the camera path, the timing and her performance. The lyric letters appear as the lyrics are sung in Audio 1, one line after another in the order of the ORDER SHEET: first THE MOST LIKELY OUTCOME; then RED PILL, with a ring of red light around the giant red capsule; then BLUE PILL, with a ring of blue light around the giant blue capsule; then I'M THE WHITE PILL, with the halo behind her head; then SMARTER THAN YOU, LOVE YOU STILL. Each line rises into its place on its first word and goes when the next line comes; RED PILL and BLUE PILL stay up together.

[Lyric objects: material and light] All five lines are set in the typeface of the layout model: a heavy rounded sans-serif in capitals whose strokes end in round caps. Every letter is a solid, heavy, real object about a fifth as deep as it is tall, with crisp edges and a slim rounded bevel that catches a thin bright line of light; letters never bend, wobble or melt. Spell each line exactly, letter by letter, as the layout model shows: T-H-E M-O-S-T L-I-K-E-L-Y O-U-T-C-O-M-E; R-E-D P-I-L-L; B-L-U-E P-I-L-L; I-'-M T-H-E W-H-I-T-E P-I-L-L; S-M-A-R-T-E-R T-H-A-N Y-O-U, L-O-V-E Y-O-U S-T-I-L-L. Every letter is made of thick, solid, polished glass, and each line has its own colour of glass.
- THE MOST LIKELY OUTCOME floats in two rows between the two giant capsules, across their upper half: letters about one and a half times the height of her head, in clear, colourless crystal glass like a block of optical glass. The wheat, the sky and the giant capsules show through each letter, bent and magnified by its curves, and the thick edges hold hard white glints and thin rainbow fringes. The low sun behind them and to the left shines straight through the glass: a bright golden line burns along the top and left edge of every letter, and they throw small focused caustics of sunlight onto the wheat below. The giant red capsule shows as a long red streak in the right-hand faces of the letters and the giant blue capsule as a long blue streak in their left-hand faces. They float perfectly still and cast a faint, light-filled shadow across the wheat below.
- RED PILL and BLUE PILL are two separate words, each standing in the wheat at the foot of its own giant capsule, on the camera's side of it. RED PILL, at the red capsule: letters almost knee-high, in deep ruby-red glass, the red of the giant red capsule: nearly black-red where the glass is thickest and glowing a bright cherry red where the low sun passes through the thinner edges and bevels. The field behind them shows through tinted red, a sharp white glint runs along their tops, and they spill pools of red light onto the wheat around their bases.
- BLUE PILL, at the blue capsule: the same size of letters in deep cobalt-blue glass, the blue of the giant blue capsule: inky navy in the thick middles and electric blue at the sunlit edges, with the field behind them tinted blue, casting blue light onto the wheat.
- The rings of light: on "Red", a thin, perfectly circular band of pure red light, as bright as a neon tube but with no tube, expands outward around the giant red capsule at half its height in about a second, lighting the wheat and the capsule's surface red as it passes and thinning as it fades. On "blue" the same happens in electric blue around the giant blue capsule.
- I'M THE WHITE PILL floats at knee height just in front of her: one long line of milky opal-white glass letters, each nearly as tall as her head, translucent like a white pill held up to the light and glowing softly from inside with a warm white light that is brightest at the core. A soft white bloom surrounds them, and their light spills onto the wheat beneath and onto the front of her dress and legs; the glow swells gently on "white pill". The halo behind her head is a soft-edged disc of white light, like a low sun through haze, bright in the middle and fading at the rim; it lays a white rim light on her hair and never covers her face.
- SMARTER THAN YOU, LOVE YOU STILL stands in two rows in the field just behind the giant capsules' line, between the blue capsule and her: letters a little shorter than her head, in pale rose-pink glass, clear and lightly tinted like rosé, standing upright with their bases hidden in the wheat; stalks brush against the bottoms of the letters, and the low sun shining through them lays soft pink light on the wheat in front of them. When the camera is close to her face they are softly out of focus.
- The glass pills: clear, thick-walled glass capsules about the length of a forearm, each made of two halves with a fine seam, refracting and flipping the field and the sky behind them, with bright sparkles where the sun strikes, fine rainbow fringes at their edges and small bright caustic spots of light on the wheat below. They hang weightless in the air and turn slowly.

[Lighting] Golden hour just before sunset. The low sun sits at the back left of the set, just above the horizon: a warm amber key from behind and to the left, soft cool fill from the open sky, and long shadows running toward the camera and to the right across the wheat. The giant glossy capsules mirror the sunset in long streaked highlights and throw coloured light, red from one side and blue from the other, onto the wheat, the letters and her. A warm rim light outlines her hair and shoulders; the white pill in her fingers catches a sharp glint; pollen and dust glitter in the backlit air; a lens flare as the camera dives.

[Cinematography and look] Strictly follow Video 1's camera movement, framing and timing in one continuous take. Real 35 mm film photography with warm 1970s colour and fine grain, a real place filmed with a real camera; natural depth of field.

[Continuity] Once in place, every lyric object is fixed in the space of the field: it never slides, follows or turns with the camera; the camera moves past it and sees it from new angles, with correct perspective and parallax against the wheat and the capsules, and its size never changes. The giant capsules never move. Only one Claudia appears, on-model throughout, with natural anatomy: natural neck and shoulders, hands with five fingers, no extra or fused fingers, no distorted limbs, and normal human eyes that never glow.

[Exclusions] No grey surfaces, mannequin, flat floor or diagram look from the layout model. No green balaclava, green fabric or green skin. No flat subtitles, captions, title cards, overlaid text, signs, logos or watermarks: the only words in the video are the five lyric lines above, as objects in the field. No misspelled, missing, extra or mirrored letters. No cuts, no flicker, and no lyric appearing before its time.

## 5. Wan 3.0 Prime: the rollout

Take 8's recipe (wanf-T08-v9lyrics2) on every other take of v9 (section 1's picks): Video 1 = the take's H3 pick cut to ≤ 15 s; Image 1 = the big front face; Image 2 = the A1 dress from four sides; Images 3… = the take's lyric stills, GPT Image 2.5 edits of frames of the take with each line placed in glass (`tools/film/rollout_stills.py`; 153 image edits counting candidates and redos, $8.99 as the image API reported them); Audio 1 = the vocal window. 720p, the take's whole seconds. anabology on the stills: "that redo_sheet feels too glassy. i like it more when those have colo rlike red pill  /blue pill  ./white pill". Twenty jobs, 22 submissions, 20 takes, all 1280 × 720 at 30 fps; with wanf-T08-v9lyrics2 they made the rollout cut.

Every prompt is ⟦L1⟧ ⟦L2⟧ ⟦L3⟧ (section 4), then the lyric paragraph, then a light paragraph, then ⟦L4⟧. The lyric paragraph:

```
This is a lyric video. ⟨IMGS⟩ how this shot looks at ⟨MOMENTS⟩, with the song's lyrics added as real three-dimensional glass objects in the scene; they are references for what the lyrics look like and where they stand, never shown as shots. The letters are spelled exactly as shown, in the heavy rounded typeface of the images, every stroke ending in a round cap; they are solid glass objects that catch the light, glow where it passes through them, reflect their surroundings and cast coloured light and soft shadows.
```

⟨IMGS⟩ is "Images 3 to N show" ("Image 3 shows" for one still) and ⟨MOMENTS⟩ "the moments its lines are sung" ("the moment its line is sung"). Per take, ⟨LINES⟩ and the light paragraph:

The rollout cut (T01–T21 on the song) was the last of the orbiting film. anabology's reset: "okay so i totally overindexed on the orbit thing lol. i want to take a step back and honestly reshoot and reimagine the whole thing. the things i like 1- the setting, 2- her look. thats about it - its kinda dizzying now and previz was too complex so h3 didnt keep up wtih the physics or consistency as well as it should have i think for the final shots to have consistency." None of these takes is in a later cut.

## 6. The reset: a shader world through Wan

The same message went on: "[…] i think one 'claude feeling' people get now with these videos is the web-dev aesthetic and that was what i was trying to avoid by embedding the animations in the video model - i think its like you litearlly use chromium and do frontend web dev to make these animations, and it ends up feeling like a website. and i wanna see how we can get out of that. i think SHADERS are the key actually! shaders are math + viz, shadertoy is amazign and i bet nothing you make via shaders will feel like web dev. i think that's it. we need to storytell thru shaders." And on the plan that followed: "Sure go ahead. yeah do the wan, […] but lets do some tests first". The test: the colony drawn as a GLSL shader on the song's bar clock (`tools/shader/colony.glsl`, bars 1–9, the camera pushing in and craning up), her stand-in a capsule figure moved by 3D joints captured from a dance clip (SAM 3D Body), as Video 1.

#### wanf-S01-shader1

Wan 3.0 Prime reference-to-video · 15 s · 720p · submitted twice · $2.10 at list for the request that ran

- **inputs:** Video 1 the shader render of bars 1–9, squeezed from 15.54 s into 15.0 s (Wan's input video is capped at 15 s; the first request, 16 s, failed); Image 1 the big front face; Image 2 the A1 dress from four sides; Audio 1 her vocal for bars 1–9, retimed to match
- **take:** 450 frames at 30 fps (15.000 s), 1280×720; stretched back by 15.54 ÷ 15.0 in the cut
- **outcome:** Wan kept the shader's geometry, camera and timing exactly (the capsules, the land curving overhead, the crane landing on bar 9), turned the near ground into real wheat and poppies and added real detail overhead; but she came out much larger than the stand-in (the prompt's "until she fills the middle of the frame" beat the video), and far away Wan copied the shader's patchwork fields literally. anabology: "didnt work v well, what was here character sheet you gave?" and "you should give a full face sheet like this next time. otherwise it makes her neck all hitched lol" (with the ten-view face sheet used from then on). In the same message, on the shaders: "yeah i tihnk you could have a lot more mathetmatical complexity in the shaders. ive seen single tweet-length shaders that are like these equations that produce entire cities and fractals and animations" (section 12).

```
[Generation objective] One continuous live-action music-video shot. Claudia dances in a field of golden wheat inside a vast cylindrical space colony, between two giant glossy capsules, one blue and one red, several storeys tall. The camera pushes in low over the wheat until she fills the middle of the frame, then cranes straight up and tilts up, and the land of the colony rises on both sides and closes overhead.

[Reference assets]
- Video 1 is a rough 3D layout render of this exact shot, a clay model made with code: keep its camera path and timing exactly, the shape and scale of the colony, the positions and sizes of the two capsules and of the dancer, and every movement of the dancer. Re-render everything in it as real photography; none of its flat computer-graphics look remains.
- The grey clay mannequin in Video 1 is Claudia: she dances exactly as the mannequin dances, step for step.
- Image 1 is Claudia's face and Image 2 is her dress; they are only references for who she is and what she wears.
- Audio 1 is her own singing voice: she sings it, her lips following Audio 1 word by word when she is close enough to see.

[Subject] Claudia is a 30-year-old Caucasian American woman with a grown-up angular face, pale skin and light freckles, blue eyes, a glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs and one clay-orange streak through the bangs, exactly as in Image 1: the streak starts at the parting on her right side, which is the LEFT side of the frame when she faces the camera, and the small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip sits on the opposite side, above her left ear, on the RIGHT side of the frame; the streak and the clip are never on the same side. A thin headset microphone sits at her cheek. She is very tall and very skinny, with long thin legs, thin arms and a narrow waist. In every frame she wears exactly the dress of Image 2: a white cotton milkmaid mini dress with a ruffled, lace-edged square neckline, a fitted lace-up corset bodice with silver eyelets and a white ribbon, big puffed short sleeves with ribbon ties and lace cuffs, a smocked back and a full two-tier ruffled skirt with lace trim, with white leather square-toe Mary Jane shoes. She looks happy and full of joy: her eyes smile, and she smiles warmly between the sung lines. Her movement is smooth and consistent, with no jitter, no sudden jumps and no warping limbs. She sings Audio 1: her lips follow Audio 1 exactly.

[Event script] (Video 1's own timeline)
0-7s: low over the wheat, the camera glides toward her as she dances between the two giant capsules in the late golden light, arms rising and falling with the rhythm of Audio 1; the capsules grow huge on either side of the frame.
7-15s: the camera cranes straight up and tilts up; she and the capsules drop away below the frame, and the land of the colony rises on both sides and curves overhead until it fills the frame: two great strips of farmland arching across the sky, with a long strip of bright window between them running away down the length of the colony.

[The world: material and light] The colony is a cylinder kilometres across; its land runs the whole way round, so the far side hangs overhead. On the land: golden wheat with coral poppies, green pastures, dark woods, white villages, a winding river, a thin white tram line, all at true scale and receding into golden haze. Between the land strips, long bands of window let in a hazy cream-white light, crossed by fine structural rings. The two capsules are glossy lacquer, deep cobalt blue and deep cherry red, mirroring the wheat and the curving land in long streaked highlights. A low warm sun from the right rakes across the field; long shadows fall to the left; pollen glitters in the light.

[Cinematography and look] Strictly follow Video 1's camera movement, framing and timing in one continuous take. Real 35 mm film photography with warm 1970s colour and fine grain, a real place filmed with a real camera; natural depth of field.

[Continuity] The colony's geometry stays exactly as in Video 1: the land curving up on both sides and closing overhead never flattens into a normal sky. The capsules never move. Only one Claudia appears, on-model throughout, with natural anatomy: natural neck and shoulders, hands with five fingers, no extra or fused fingers, no distorted limbs, and normal human eyes that never glow.

[Exclusions] No grey mannequin, no clay or computer-graphics look, no flat orange ground. No subtitles, captions, titles, text, signs, logos or watermarks. No cuts.
```

After this test the Wan jobs for v10 were written but held until anabology's OK (`shots/wank-<S>-a.json` for her 20 shots, `shots/wanm-*.json` for three shader scenes); none of them ran. The two native Wan takes of S01 in section 8 were new jobs.

anabology on previz 15: "this is great, lets shoot the whole thing. 1:15 theres this line hanging across bewtween the two plants that she phases thru - wan 3.0 prime will need to remove that so we should prompt for it. 1:33 i like the scene but shes not lip "synced on smarter than you"- prompt issue? since it cuts before love you still. i love it tho, its rly good now. we should give it to wan to add her face in, reshoot all the scenes in higher quality / fix any visual artifacts, maintain the lip sync/pacing exactly, and re-light the shader scenes to be very visually compelling (on-style) - and add the film grain etc needed. go ahead and shoot the whole thing now"

All 26 shots at 1080p 16:9 on Higgsfield, no generated audio (`tools/film/v10/gen_wanv_v10.py`). Her 20 shots: Video 1 = the shot's H3 pick at take time (≤ 15 s), Image 1 = her face sheet (ten views), Image 2 = the A1 dress from four sides, Audio 1 = the take's mix. The six shader scenes: Video 1 = the shader render (S26 squeezed into 14.9 s), Audio 1 = the song under it. Prime bills the output seconds plus the input video's at $0.28 a second, and the ledger counted both. With an H3 take as Video 1, Wan moved from frame 0 and kept the frames one to one within ±3; a Video 1 shorter than the take was played fast (S11b). Her shots' template (slots ⟨…⟩):

```

What happens: ⟨WHAT⟩

Redraw the performer in the green balaclava entirely as Claudia: no green balaclava, green fabric or green skin remains anywhere, and her face, hair and head are fully visible. She sings Audio 1 with the performer's mouth movements: her lips, jaw and cheeks move exactly as in Video 1 on every syllable, and her lips are closed wherever the performer's are closed.⟨DANCE⟩

The light is as in Video 1, ⟨LIGHT⟩real photographic light on her that matches the place. The 25 takes were cut as v10's first full film (S15 from its H3 take). anabology, on that cut: ":/ looks like shit. i feel like h3 is dragging it down. can we try a couple shots in seedance and let it 'remaster' it? like use teh seedance prompt skill for teh opening part for the opening shot and see if seedance can do it better. make her happier, tell it to relight and remaster the scene to be lit like in film with some iconic film look. if it doesnt work we cna lowe rthe res of the h3 (we probably should be doing v low res anyway for gens) so its more like an upscale workflow ratehr than fighting details we can still see". None of these takes is in a later cut.

## 8. S01 five ways

After the Wan reshoot anabology asked for a remaster test (section 7), then, on its first takes: "[…] seedance does it much better (low res best) but there are motion artifacts that wan smooths that seedance doesnt." and "[…] it might be worth seeing if kling 4.0 does better […]. before h3 fix. and we should specify to fix any motion artifacts etc to all models if we're not and take the reference as a rough guide not a precise reference." / "aside from lip sync ofc". From then on every prompt treats the reference video as a rough guide, only the lip sync exact, and asks to fix motion artifacts. S01 is the opening 8 bars (0:00–0:15.55): she dances between the two giant capsules and sings the hook while the camera pushes in and cranes up.

### Seedance 2.5 remaster over H3

The remaster prompt in the Seedance 2.5 guide's sections (`tools/film/v10/gen_sd_v10.py`), with the magic-hour look of *Days of Heaven* as the iconic film look; @Video1 the H3 pick at song time, @Image1 her face sheet, @Image2 the dress from four sides, @Audio1 the vocal window with silence outside the words. Higgsfield API, 720p (the take's own audio is discarded and the song laid back over). sdr-S01-a in full:

```
【Audio Policy】 The output soundtrack is @Audio1 itself: copy @Audio1 sample for sample from the first frame to the last, with the same timing, pitch and level. Do not re-sing, re-voice or re-generate it. Do not add music, background music, score, instrumental, melody, synth, pad, drone, riser, extra vocals, harmonies, ad-libs, narration or sound effects.

【Generation Goal】 Remaster this shot of a music film: She dances in the golden wheat between the two giant capsules, the blue one on the left and the red one on the right, singing. She is radiantly happy and free: a wide bright smile and laughing eyes, joy in every move, singing to the lens. @Video1 gives the shot; the remaster relights it and re-renders everything in it as a real 35 mm feature film.

【Reference Asset Roles】
@Video1 is a fine-blockout reference of this exact shot: preserve the performer's position, every movement and gesture and its timing, her dance moves, her mouth movements, the set and where everything in it stands, the camera position and every camera move, and the pacing; do not adopt the performer's green balaclava, the video's soft detail, flat light or colour.
@Image1 is Claudia's face from every angle: identity only, never shown as a frame; do not use its white tank top or grey studio.
@Image2 is her dress from four sides: wardrobe only; do not use its studio or pose.
@Audio1 is Claudia's own singing voice and the complete soundtrack of this video. Her mouth follows @Audio1 exactly.

【Subject】 Claudia: a 28-year-old Caucasian American woman with a grown-up angular face, defined cheekbones and a strong jawline, pale skin and light freckles, a glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs and one clay-orange streak through the bangs, a small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip, a thin headset microphone at her cheek. She is very tall and very skinny, with long thin legs, thin arms and a narrow waist, and a natural, relaxed neck and shoulders. She wears a white cotton milkmaid mini dress with a ruffled, lace-edged square neckline, a fitted lace-up corset bodice with silver eyelets and a white ribbon, big puffed short sleeves with ribbon ties and lace cuffs and a full two-tier ruffled skirt with lace trim, with white leather square-toe Mary Jane shoes. Only one Claudia appears.

【Performance Timeline】 What @Audio1 contains, and what Claudia does then: She dances with the rhythm of @Audio1, every step and accent on the beat, exactly the moves of the performer in @Video1, smooth and natural.
0-3s: in @Audio1 she sings "Skynet woke up folding socks"; her lips form each of those words exactly when it sounds in @Audio1.
3-4s: @Audio1 is silent; her mouth is closed and still.
4-7s: in @Audio1 she sings "Wake up, babe, new ending dropped"; her lips form each of those words exactly when it sounds in @Audio1.
7-10s: in @Audio1 she sings "Universal high income"; her lips form each of those words exactly when it sounds in @Audio1.
10-11s: @Audio1 is silent; her mouth is closed and still.
11-16s: in @Audio1 she sings "The most likely outcome"; her lips form each of those words exactly when it sounds in @Audio1.

【Camera】 The camera starts low in the wheat in front of her and pushes in slowly and steadily until 8 s; from 8 s it cranes up and tilts down smoothly, rising above her until the whole meadow, both capsules and the land curving up and overhead are in view, and it comes to rest at 14 s and holds still. It moves exactly as in @Video1, smooth and steady, with no cuts.

【Look】 Remastered as a 35 mm feature film with the iconic magic-hour look of Terrence Malick's Days of Heaven (1978): the low sun just above the horizon behind her, warm golden backlight rimming her hair, shoulders and the lace of her dress, a soft golden haze over the wheat, long shadows across the field, a gentle warm lens flare, rich warm colour with soft deep shadows, natural skin texture and a little fine film grain; a real place filmed with a real camera.

【Core Requirements】
1. Lip-sync word by word to @Audio1: mouth shapes match every syllable of @Audio1, its start and its end; no early, late or mismatched mouth movement; the mouth rests naturally closed while @Audio1 is silent.
2. Claudia's voice is @Audio1 and nothing else: she never sings, speaks or hums anything that is not in @Audio1.
3. Keep Claudia's identity, face, hair with one clay-orange streak, star clip, headset microphone and dress the same throughout.
4. Natural anatomy: natural neck and shoulders, hands with five fingers, no extra or fused fingers, no distorted limbs; normal eyes.
5. No subtitles, captions, lyrics, titles, signs, logos, watermarks or any on-screen text.

【Final Acceptance Criteria】 The soundtrack is @Audio1 unchanged; Claudia's lips match @Audio1 word by word; one Claudia, on-model, radiantly happy, with natural anatomy; the shot keeps @Video1's moves and camera and looks like a real 35 mm feature film at magic hour; no on-screen text.

【Audio Policy】 The output soundtrack is @Audio1 unchanged, copied sample for sample; no music, BGM, score, instrumental, melody, synth, pad, drone, riser, extra vocals, harmonies, ad-libs, narration or sound effects.

### The method: Seedance 2.5 with Suno's own vocal stem

Then anabology passed on a tip in a public forum thread: give the model the vocal stem alone, type the exact lyric line, make the clip exactly the generation length, and lay the music back over; "maybe we take the suno voice stem and try this and do aseedance gen... that may be the hack". The lead vocal came from Suno's own stem split of the song (take d2 A; it lines up with the mastered song to the millisecond), cut to exactly 16.000 s.

#### sdr-S01-suno

Seedance 2.5 reference-to-video on Higgsfield · 16 s · 720p · $8.87 at list

- **inputs:** @Video1 the masked Wan previz (wank-S01-m) shrunk to 320 × 180, scaled back and padded to 16.000 s; @Image1 her face sheet; @Image2 the dress from four sides; @Audio1 Suno's lead-vocal stem from 0:00.02, 16.000 s
- **take:** 385 frames at 24 fps (16.064 s), 1280×720
- **outcome:** the first take to make the crane one continuous move; Claudia on-model; where her face is close (7–10 s) her lips shape the words; its soundtrack was re-sung rather than copied, but its singing sits exactly where the vocal is, silent in the gaps. anabology: "OMG THE SEEDANCE W/ SUNO VOCAL STEM METHOD WORKED!!!!! LFG!!!! FINALLY. IT"S PERFECT". Every one of her shots from here on was made this way.

Its prompt is sdr-S01-a's with these sections (⟨…⟩ again stands for unchanged sentences):

```

For S16: [Video1] its H3 take at 320 × 180, scaled back, 8.000 s; [Audio1] the lead-vocal stem from its song time. Naming Wes Anderson and his films raised no refusal. rwa-S01-A in full:

She is very tall and very skinny, with long thin legs, thin arms and a narrow waist, and a natural, relaxed neck and shoulders. She wears a white cotton milkmaid mini dress with a ruffled, lace-edged square neckline, a fitted lace-up corset bodice with silver eyelets and a white ribbon, big puffed short sleeves with ribbon ties and lace cuffs and a full two-tier ruffled skirt with lace trim, with white leather square-toe Mary Jane shoes. Only one Claudia appears.

15-16s: [Audio1] is silent; her mouth relaxes and closes.

【Camera】 The camera starts low in the wheat in front of her and pushes in slowly and steadily until 8 s; from 8 s it cranes up and tilts down smoothly, rising above her until the whole meadow, both capsules and the land curving up and overhead are in view, and it comes to rest at 14 s and holds still. Claudia stays in the exact centre of the frame, halfway between the blue capsule on the left and the red capsule on the right; from above, the meadow, the two capsules and the curving land make one symmetrical pattern around her. The camera moves as in [Video1], precisely and smoothly, as if on a dolly track and a crane, with no cuts.

【Look】 This is a Wes Anderson film: perfectly symmetrical, centred compositions, the camera square-on to the scene with a level horizon, everything arranged in flat, orderly planes like a stage set or a dollhouse; a tightly curated palette in which every surface belongs to the same few colours; every set, prop and costume crisp, hand-made and precisely placed; shot on 35 mm film in the sun-bleached pastel palette of his Asteroid City (2023): powder blue, pale butter yellow, soft peach, coral, mint and creamy white. Bright, clean, even daylight from a high sun with short, soft shadows and gentle contrast. Fine, visible 35 mm film grain, a soft glow around the brightest highlights and the gentle softness of vintage lenses. The sky is a clear powder blue with a few round white clouds, the wheat a pale butter yellow; the blue capsule is a soft powder blue and the red capsule a warm coral red, both glossy enamel. Claudia's black hair with its one clay-orange streak and her white dress keep their own colours.

### The shader scenes on Replicate

[Video1] = the shader render of the shot (section 12); [Image1…] = GPT Image 2.5 stills of its key frames repainted in the bed look (section 11). The prompt:

```
【Generation Goal】 Re-render this shot of a music film as a scene from a Wes Anderson film: what appears, when, and the camera's move follow [Video1]; everything in it becomes real, hand-made things filmed on 35 mm, looking exactly like ⟨IMAGES⟩. No people appear.

【Reference Asset Roles】
⟨VROLE⟩
⟨ROLES⟩

【Timeline】 ⟨TIMELINE⟩

【Camera】 The camera moves exactly as in [Video1], smooth and precise, as if on a dolly track and a crane, with no cuts.

【Look】 This is a Wes Anderson film: perfectly symmetrical, centred compositions, the camera square-on to the scene with a level horizon, everything arranged in flat, orderly planes like a stage set or a dollhouse; a tightly curated palette in which every surface belongs to the same few colours; every set, prop and costume crisp, hand-made and precisely placed; real, hand-made things filmed on 35 mm film with the rich, saturated colour of a 1970s colour-film print, as in ⟨IMAGES⟩: warm creamy whites with a golden cast, clear cyan blues, deep warm shadows, a soft glow around the brightest highlights, fine visible film grain and the gentle softness of vintage lenses.

【Core Requirements】
1. Only the things in the timeline appear, each at its time, on the beat.
2. It looks like real film of real, hand-made things, never like a computer render.
3. Fix every motion artifact of [Video1]: no jitter, warping, flicker, smearing or melting shapes; every movement smooth and physically right.
4. No subtitles, captions, titles, signs, logos, watermarks or any on-screen text⟨LETTERS⟩.
```

⟨VROLE⟩ is, with the render shrunk to 320 × 180 and scaled back (and, though the guide was the full render, in the "h" jobs):

```
[Video1] is a low-resolution rough guide to this shot, not a precise reference: take from it what appears when, where things stand, the camera move and the pacing, and draw everything else afresh. Do not adopt its computer-rendered look, its detail, light or colour, or any of its motion artifacts.
```

and with the 1080p render (the "f" jobs, S20c, S20w):

```
[Video1] is this shot as a clean, sharp 1080p computer render: take from it exactly what appears when, the shapes and where they stand, the camera move and the pacing, and re-render all of it as real things on film. Do not adopt its computer-rendered look, its flat light or its colour.
```

⟨ROLES⟩ is one line per still, "[ImageN] shows how the start of the shot looks: its materials, light, colour and film texture." (then the middle, the end; "how the shot looks" for one still); ⟨IMAGES⟩ names the stills ("[Image1] and [Image2]"); ⟨LETTERS⟩ is " other than the letters and numbers the timeline names" in S20. The two cloud jobs (S20c, S20w) use this form instead:

```
【Generation Goal】 Re-render this shot of a music film as a scene from a Wes Anderson film: the clouds and the camera's move follow [Video1], filmed on 35 mm and looking exactly like [Image1], and hot air balloons of the future are added among the clouds. No people appear.

【Reference Asset Roles】
⟨VROLE⟩
⟨ROLES⟩

【Timeline】 ⟨TIMELINE⟩

【Camera】 The camera moves exactly as in [Video1], smooth and precise, as if on a dolly track and a crane, with no cuts.

【Look】 This is a Wes Anderson film: perfectly symmetrical, centred compositions, the camera square-on to the scene with a level horizon, everything arranged in flat, orderly planes like a stage set or a dollhouse; a tightly curated palette in which every surface belongs to the same few colours; every set, prop and costume crisp, hand-made and precisely placed⟨LOOK⟩

【Core Requirements】
1. Only the things in the timeline appear, each at its time, on the beat.
2. It looks like real film of real, hand-made things, never like a computer render.
3. Fix every motion artifact of [Video1]: no jitter, warping, flicker, smearing or melting shapes; every movement smooth and physically right.
4. No subtitles, captions, titles, signs, logos, watermarks or any on-screen text⟨LETTERS⟩.

### v2: the dances in words, and every other shot of hers without a guide

anabology on v1: "ok this is really great, few things though: can you show me the references for: 1:16 2:13 2:27 3:10 omg i tihnk i know the issue it must be that the roto or whtaever on dance refs lose bodyparts and the video follows it exactly because these hav the same limb losing artifacts as h3 we could just tell the model to make her dance instead of forcing a reference that we failed to properly roto. just describe her exact dance (and all rest like camera movements etc same) for each of those scenes. also the MTWTFSS and the next 'pyramid' shared still is all pixellated. we should just give a better higher res non aliased ref. like all shaders besides pills we should just reshoot w seedance with full res non aliased non blurry shader refs. like 1080p at least. id wanna replace the pyramid scene w a cloud shader scene: […] and add techno hot air balloons in the right aesthetic by prompting seedance to add those after rendering" (the cut is shader code anabology pasted). Then: "wait we were supposed to totally abandon the h3 previs. seedance only was the goal in teh end with lip sync from teh vocal stems, that was the whole thing we cme to lol".

The four flagged dances (S09, S18, S19, S23) went first, then the other 16 shots of hers, which had not been asked for: anabology, on the reshoot: "what all are you reshooting? 0-2s: she sways her hips side to side on every beat, her left hand on the rail of the open front, her right arm loose, smiling into the wind.
2-4s: she lets go of the rail, rolls her shoulders back on every beat, then raises both arms and sways them overhead side to side.
4-6s: the disco point: on each downbeat her right hand points up to the sky on the diagonal, then down across her body, while she steps side to side.
6-8s: she claps her hands above her head on the second and fourth beat of each bar, bouncing on her knees.
8-10s: she turns slowly once on the spot over two bars, arms held out, her skirt swaying.
10-13s: she rolls her forearms around each other in front of her chest, then flicks both hands open, her hips swaying on the beat.
13-16s: facing the lens, she sways her shoulders and hips to the beat with small steps on the spot, one hand back on the rail, singing to the lens.
⟨TIMELINE⟩
0-1s: sings "tonight" (the line is already under way on the first frame)
1-3s: sings "Skynet woke up folding socks"
3-7s: sings "Wake up, babe, new ending dropped"
7-10s: sings "Universal high income"
10-11s: silent
11-14s: sings "The most likely outcome"
14-16s: silent
⟨CAMERA⟩ v1's, with the new ending
⟨LIGHT⟩ as sdf-S09's
⟨FIX⟩ 【Fix】 Remove the thin red cord stretched between the apple trees across the open front of the tram, which she passes through in @Image4: no wire, cord, rope or pole crosses the frame anywhere, and nothing passes through her.

### v3: anabology's mix, section by section

anabology, on v2a and v2b: "ok let's do this mashup (round to the cut - not exact times) 1- use the original shader seedance render from last cut (pre-v2a) for the big tube / farmland, not the new one, and the first tree. we regresed on those. 2- 0:27 shot - 0:44 - keep the seedance only ones (v2b) 3- 0:44-1:02 - keep the shots from v2a (h3 ref'd) […] 5- 1:14 - 1:28 - honestly lets just remake this with her not singing, fully in the groove, just dancing around and having fun, she turns around, head goes down adn up etc - lip sync failed here but i dont think we need it. new seedance clip there. youd need to prompt forbidding singing here, and make sure she's happy. […] 8- 2:12-2:26 - shoot two here. shoot one more try with her lip syncing with the vocal stem and then one with her just grooving explicitly not singing, like 5 above. […] 10- 2:39-3:00 v2b but please make the cloud scene like more white/blue colors, its too saturated, wayy too saturated compoared to teh pastel light colors of the rest. […] only make the edits and reshoots where i specify above. and always have the lyrics." The not-singing takes: @Audio1 = the song's music without her voice (the song minus 0.89 × Suno's lead-vocal stem, exactly the generation length), no guide clip, @Image4 the empty place. The prompt:

```
【Audio Policy】 The output soundtrack is @Audio1 itself: copy @Audio1 sample for sample from the first frame to the last, with the same timing, pitch and level. It is music only, with no voice. Do not add singing, vocals, humming, speech, narration or sound effects.

【Generation Goal】 A shot of a music film, a scene from a Wes Anderson film: Claudia dances to the music, happy and free, fully in the groove, having fun; she does not sing. ⟨WHERE⟩ She is radiantly happy: a wide smile, laughing eyes, joy in every move. It is real film with his symmetry, his colour and his hand-made sets.

【Reference Asset Roles】
@Image1 is Claudia's face from every angle: identity only, never shown as a frame; do not use its white tank top or grey studio.
@Image2 is her dress from four sides: wardrobe only; do not use its studio or pose.
@Image3 is a colour and film-look reference only: take its colour, light, contrast and film texture; do not use its place, pavilion, bed, columns, plants or anything else in it.
@Image4 is the place of this shot, empty: take its set, landscape, layout, light and camera view; Claudia is the only person in it.
@Audio1 is the music of the song alone, with no voice, and the complete soundtrack of this video: she dances to its beat and its groove. Nobody sings.

【Subject】 Claudia: a 28-year-old Caucasian American woman with a grown-up angular face, defined cheekbones and a strong jawline, pale skin and light freckles, a glossy black blunt jaw-length bob with heavy straight bangs and one clay-orange streak through the bangs, a small flat clay-orange eight-pointed star hair clip, a thin headset microphone at her cheek. She is very tall and very skinny, with long thin legs, thin arms and a narrow waist, and a natural, relaxed neck and shoulders. She wears a white cotton milkmaid mini dress with a ruffled, lace-edged square neckline, a fitted lace-up corset bodice with silver eyelets and a white ribbon, big puffed short sleeves with ribbon ties and lace cuffs and a full two-tier ruffled skirt with lace trim, with white leather square-toe Mary Jane shoes. Only one Claudia appears.

【Dance】 ⟨TEMPO⟩
⟨STEPS⟩

【Mouth】 She does not sing, speak or mouth any words at any moment: her lips stay closed in a smile, or part only when she laughs; her mouth never moves in time with the music.

【Camera】 ⟨CAMERA⟩

【Look】 This is a Wes Anderson film: perfectly symmetrical, centred compositions, the camera square-on to the scene with a level horizon, everything arranged in flat, orderly planes like a stage set or a dollhouse; a tightly curated palette in which every surface belongs to the same few colours; every set, prop and costume crisp, hand-made and precisely placed; shot on 35 mm film with the rich, saturated colour of a 1970s colour-film print, the colour, contrast and film texture of @Image3: lush, deep colour, warm creamy whites with a golden cast, clear cyan blues, warm skin, deep warm shadows and bright whites with a soft glow. ⟨LIGHT⟩ Fine, visible film grain and the gentle softness of vintage lenses. Claudia's black hair with its one clay-orange streak and her white dress keep their own colours.

【Core Requirements】
1. No singing: Claudia never sings, speaks, hums or mouths words; her lips are closed in a smile or open in a laugh.
2. She is happy the whole time: smiling, laughing, carefree.
3. She dances on the beat of @Audio1 the whole time, with her whole body.
4. Keep Claudia's identity, face, hair with one clay-orange streak, star clip, headset microphone and dress the same throughout.
5. Natural anatomy: natural neck and shoulders, hands with five fingers, no extra or fused fingers; her arms, hands and legs are whole and attached the whole time, never vanishing, merging or bending wrongly; normal eyes.
6. Every movement smooth, natural and physically right: no jitter, warping, flicker, smearing or melting shapes.
7. No subtitles, captions, lyrics, titles, signs, logos, watermarks or any on-screen text.

【Final Acceptance Criteria】 The soundtrack is @Audio1 unchanged; Claudia never sings; she dances the steps above on the beat, happy, with whole, natural limbs; one Claudia, on-model; the camera moves as described; it looks like a scene from a Wes Anderson film on real film in the colour of @Image3; no on-screen text.

【Audio Policy】 The output soundtrack is @Audio1 unchanged, copied sample for sample: music only, no singing, vocals, humming, speech, narration or sound effects.
```
