## 11. The stills that fed the video jobs (GPT Image 2.5)

Every still here is an edit by GPT Image 2.5 (Sunburst) through an image API: the instruction is sent after "Edit the first image. ", the first image is the frame being edited and any further images follow as references; 1824 × 1024, high quality. Costs are as the API reported them (the v10 keyframes also in `shots/ledger-img.jsonl`). The rollout's lyric stills are in section 5; the earlier outfit sheets in sections 1 and 3.

**S01, for the Wan and Seedance jobs of section 8.** The S01 keyframe ($0.07), one of the 20 keyframes made for the v10 previz (27 edits with the redos, $1.77; with three edits of a wheat plate, $1.92 in `shots/ledger-img.jsonl`): first image the empty place (a Midjourney plate of the capsules), second her face sheet, third the dress from four sides:

```
Add one woman to this place, as a still frame from a 35mm film: Claudia, the woman shown in the second image (a sheet of her face from every angle; Caucasian American, pale skin with freckles; the exact face, hair and colouring of the sheet), wearing exactly the outfit in the third image (a white cotton milkmaid mini dress with a ruffled, lace-edged square neckline, a fitted lace-up bodice, big puffed short sleeves and a two-tier ruffled skirt, with white Mary Jane shoes). She dances in the wheat between the two giant capsules, mid-step with one arm raised, smiling as she sings; full body, in the centre, about a third of the frame height. She is slim and tall with natural proportions, every limb and hand anatomically correct. Keep the place exactly as it is (its composition, objects, light and colours) and light her with the place's own light (low golden sunlight with soft haze), so she belongs in the photograph. No text, no captions.
```

The crane's end frame ($0.06), from the keyframe with the dress as the second image:

```
Show the same place from high above, as the end of a crane shot that has risen over her: looking steeply down on the golden wheat and coral poppy meadow, the two giant glossy capsules (deep blue on the left, cherry red on the right) seen from above, and the same woman in the white dress small between them, dancing, her arms raised; the meadow is divided into long fields, and at the top of the frame the land of the colony curves up into golden haze. Same low golden light, long shadows. No text.
```

The masked keyframe for wank-S01-m ($0.05):

```
Edit only her head. Cover her whole head and face, her hair, ears and neck, with a tight matte chroma-key green balaclava, a plain bright green visual-effects hood, with two small eye holes and one small mouth hole, so only her eyes and her lips show through it. Keep everything else in the image exactly the same: her pose, arms, legs and body, the white milkmaid dress and white shoes, the golden wheat and poppies, the blue capsule on the left and the red capsule on the right, the sky, the light and the composition.
```

**The look.** The crop of the bed take that set the film's colour is not a generation: the left 560 × 720 pixels of a frame of rwa-S16-A, where she is not. The two targets of rwa-S12-S ($0.05 each), from the S12 render's frames at 1.4 s and 4.8 s, with the crop as the second image; ⟨LOOK⟩ is the same paragraph in both:

```
close:
Repaint it as a frame from a 1970s 35 mm feature film: a real, hand-made model photographed on film. Keep the composition exactly: the big clear glass capsule with rounded ends in the middle of the frame at the same size and position; inside it a bright blue sky with soft white clouds along the bottom and the small white two-piece capsule colonies floating in the same places, each with three tiny warm-lit window strips, and at the centre one smaller glass capsule holding its own tiny world; behind it deep black space with faint stars and, at the very bottom, the thin bright curve of the Earth's atmosphere. ⟨LOOK⟩

wide:
Repaint it as a frame from a 1970s 35 mm feature film: a real, hand-made model photographed on film. Keep the composition exactly: the glass capsule small near the top centre with its blue sky, clouds and tiny white capsule colonies inside; below it the curve of the Earth at dawn, its thin bright atmosphere glowing warm orange on the left and cyan blue on the right, the night side of the Earth deep blue-black; deep black space with faint stars above. ⟨LOOK⟩

⟨LOOK⟩
Give it exactly the colour, light, contrast and film texture of the second image (only its colour and film look; none of its place or objects): the rich, saturated colour of a 1970s colour-film print, warm creamy whites with a golden cast, clear cyan blues, deep warm shadows, a soft glow around the brightest highlights, fine visible film grain and the gentle softness of a vintage lens. It must look like a real photograph of a real object made by hand, never like a computer render: thick real glass with real reflections, refraction and tiny specks of dust, lacquered white model capsules with tiny imperfections. S20c:

Among the clouds float hot air balloons of the future: round envelopes striped in soft cream, pale coral and pale sky blue, each with a band of glinting solar panels around its middle, a softly glowing burner and a small white gondola with two ducted fans; one large balloon close on the right, a few smaller ones further off toward the sun. Keep every cloud and the sun where they are. Low saturation, airy and light, fine film grain and the gentle softness of a vintage lens; it must look like a real photograph, never a computer render. No text, no people.
```

Keep everything else exactly as it is: the composition, the camera angle, the light, the colours and the film texture. No text, no people.

19 plates, $0.86.

## 12. The shader scenes

After the reset (section 6) the worlds without her were written as GLSL shaders, one iterated rule per world, light and colour from the same loop. anabology on the first ones: "ok its decent but like.. idk i feel like ive seen a lot more creativity in shaders, and completeness, in the ones i sent even. clouds, cities, etc made extremely compelling with short shaders."; then "Idk if feels like you're adding ... items to increase complexity rather than compressing intricacy? Does that make sense? What's the difference between your shader code and the examples I gave"; "Go ahead, try to make something sophisticated and beautiful"; and "[…] remember the two key things here are the shader compressed intricacy combined with the audio lessons (structuring the film based on the audio from the start)".

**How they were rendered.** `tools/shader/render.py` draws a fragment shader headless on a GPU (EGL through moderngl), either a Shadertoy-style `mainImage()` or a compact twigl-style body (`FC`, `r`, `t`, `o`, `rotate2D`, `rotate3D`, `hsv`, `snoise3D`). Its clock is the song's: song seconds, plus the bar and beat positions from the beat map (bar 39.0 is the downbeat of bar 39), so every move is written in bars and lands on a downbeat. Frames are supersampled, drawn in tiles, box-filtered and piped to ffmpeg with the song under the same window. The previz renders were 1280 × 720 at 24 fps; for the final shoot the scenes were rendered again at 1920 × 1080 with 3 × 3 supersampling in 3 × 3 tiles on a render box. Seedance got them three ways: shrunk to 320 × 180 and scaled back (v1), the 1280 × 720 render as it is (the "h" jobs) and the 1080p render (the "f" jobs and the clouds).

**The worlds in the final cut.** Each went through several rounds (fold 1–6, pill 1–6, the endings tree), written by agents and reviewed against the cut on the bar map; the picks are in `tools/film/v10/m_picks.json`.

- **S02, bars 9–15, `tw2/fold6-s02.glsl` (the origami colony).** One rule: an infinite sheet folded eight times, `p = abs(mod(p + A, 2A) - A)` with long cells halving from 128 to 1, the crease relief `min(p.x, p.y)`, each level turning the levels below it on the downbeat after its relief appears; the sheet wrapped on the inside of an O'Neill cylinder, the land flattening into three ribbed window strips. Bar 9 (the bass coming in) opens with three levels in place; on each downbeat from 10 to 14 one coarser level snaps in, eased over the twelfth of a bar before it, while the camera cranes 18-fold up the colony's axis until the land closes overhead. Seedance turned it into the farming ark.
- **S12, bars 50–53, and S26, bars 115–123, `tw2/pill6-colony.glsl` (the white pill).** Every capsule holds the same nine children: a glass capsule at its centre (a quarter of its size, turned 0.2 rad) and eight white colonies scattered by a sine hash of their index. The ray counts the glass walls it is inside, and leaving a world it ends on that world's sky, so each glass pill is a sealed world; each level's sky is a step warmer (1.45 × (0.97, 0.8, 0.64) to the power of the level: white, cream, peach, amber), and the colonies are white two-piece capsules with three warm window strips. S12: the opaque pill of the bar-50 frame turns to glass by beat 3 and the camera pulls back over the Earth at dawn. S26: the camera sits on the chain's axis at a distance of 4ⁿ and leaves one glass wall exactly on each downbeat from 116 to 120, bursts out of the hull on 121 as the sun crests the limb, and the pill closes back to white.
- **S17, bars 67–69, and S21, bars 90–95, `tw2/endings.glsl` (the tree of endings).** One fold repeated 24 times (step up one unit, mirror x, turn −0.5 rad and 1.6 rad, scale by 1.25) grows a tree with 2²⁴, about 16.8 million, tips: the possible endings. S17 lights one path, hers, as the camera dives along it; S21 lights the tree from the trunk out, about one and a half levels a beat, until the crown glows, on a night meadow, the camera in a slow orbit. The same tree fits in a tweet (`tw2/tweet-tree.glsl`, one of three tweet-length versions of the worlds):

```
// twigl
vec3 d=normalize(vec3(FC.xy*2.-r,r.y*1.4)),p;d.yz*=rotate2D(-.12);for(float i,e,z,s,j,l,w,c;i++<2e2&&z<25.;z+=max(e*.6,w*.5)){p=z*d+vec3(0,2,-6.5);w=z/r.y*2.;p.y=abs(p.y);p.xz*=rotate2D(t*.1);for(e=9.,s=1.,j=0.;j++<24.;p.y-=1.,p.x=abs(p.x),p.xy*=rotate2D(-.5),p.xz*=rotate2D(1.6),p*=1.25,s*=1.25)l=(length(p-vec3(0,clamp(p.y,0.,1.),0))-.03)/s,l<e?e=l,c=j:c;o.rgb+=hsv(.08,1.-c/24.,1.)*exp(-e/w)*.02/(1.+c*.1)*(z*d.y<-2.?.25:1.);}o=tanh(o*1.3+vec4(3,1.4,.5,0)*.002/(abs(d.y)+.01));
```

- **S20, bars 83–88, `tw2/fold6-break.glsl` (the break).** One sheet of paper refolds on each sung line's downbeat into that line's object, from real data (`tools/shader/data/`): bar 83, "Proteins folded", the backbone of ubiquitin (PDB entry 1UBQ, its 76 Cα atoms, as a bas-relief whose crest height is each atom's depth); bar 84, "Old Enigma codes, decoded", the wiring of the Enigma I's rotor I, EKMFLGDQVZNTOWYHXUSPAIBRCJ, as 26 creases between two rings, so its cycles show as polygons, stepping one letter on beats 2, 3 and 4; bar 85, the letters ABC; bar 86, 42 (Deep Thought's answer); bar 87, the week as seven cells M T W T F S S, Monday's rising on beat 1, Tuesday's on beat 2, the other five folding flat on beat 3. In the shader the sheet then folds flat and the camera rises to a paper horizon at sunset (bars 88–90); in the final cut that tail is the clouds.
- **S20, bars 88–90, the cloud field.** A cloud shader anabology supplied (`tw2/clouds-field.glsl`, pasted into their notes on v1; its code is not reproduced here): a deck of cumulus flown over low at sunset. It was rendered on the same renderer at 1080p (rsf-S20c), then as a day variant (`tw2/clouds-field-day.glsl`) that changed its sun, sky and absorption so the clouds stay white under a pale blue sky, its shapes and flight unchanged (rsf-S20w). The hot air balloons are Seedance's, from the still in section 11.

The shaders' first test as a world for Wan, `tools/shader/colony.glsl` (the colony as a cylinder 6.4 km across, three land strips facing three window strips, two capsules as distance functions), is in section 6; in the Wan reshoot of section 7 the six previz renders were Wan's Video 1.
